Title: Linear-Time Context Compression with State-Space Encoding

  • Authors: Marc Serra Ortega, Andrey Barsky, Carlos Boned Riera, Tomás Ockier, Dimosthenis Karatzas
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33006
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33006
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33006]] Linear-Time Context Compression with State-Space Encoding(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33006)
  • Keywords: transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: Transformer-based large language models (LLMs) impose substantial computation and memory costs as context sizes increase, motivating methods that compress a context into a shorter representation before inference. Existing soft-prompt compression methods typically use the same Transformer backbone as used in the target model, where producing the compressed representation leads to significant latency overheads. This restricts them to the offline setting where context is compressed once and then reused across many queries. We propose the Boa Compressor, a plug-and-play online context compressor built on a Mamba state-space model (SSM) that encodes arbitrary-length context in a single linear-time pass, producing a representation on which we condition a frozen target LLM. The SSM is trained by distillation, matching the next-token logit distribution from the LLM ingesting the full context (as teacher) to the same LLM ingesting the compressed representation (as the student). At equal compression ratios, Boa matches or surpasses the performance of existing state-of-the-art offline compression baselines, while being an order of magnitude faster than Transformer-based implementations, thereby reducing end-to-end latency rather than adding compression time overhead. We demonstrate that Boa also scales well to longer contexts and to compression ratios as aggressive as 16x. By enabling effective compression that is cheap enough to run on every query at inference time, Boa becomes a practical and general tool for efficient long-context inference.

Title: Knowledge-Guided LLM for Intent-Driven Service Orchestration in Integrated Terrestrial and Non-Terrestrial 6G Networks

  • Authors: Wajdi Hammami, Irfan Azam, Soumaya Cherkaoui
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33007
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33007
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33007]] Knowledge-Guided LLM for Intent-Driven Service Orchestration in Integrated Terrestrial and Non-Terrestrial 6G Networks(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33007)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Natural-language service intents in integrated terrestrial and non-terrestrial (TN/NTN) 6G networks can be incomplete, ambiguous, or inconsistent, making unconstrained LLM generation challenging for reliable network-facing orchestration. This paper proposes a knowledge-guided LLM framework that combines grammar-constrained semantic parsing with a knowledge-graph (KG)-driven clarification loop to produce validated, orchestration-ready service profiles across four representative service classes. On a 200-sample intent dataset, the framework achieves 89% classification accuracy and 95% policy validity with an average of 0.66 clarification turns per request. Compared with a Pure-LLM baseline, the proposed framework improves policy validity and contradiction detection while requiring fewer clarification turns, demonstrating the value of explicit knowledge-guided reasoning for robust intent processing in integrated TN/NTN 6G orchestration.

Title: TraceQuery: learning what to recompute from full-denoising trajectory in diffusion language models

  • Authors: Yukai Jin, Zhankai Ye, Jiayue Yang, Fangyi Li, Shuoqiu Li, Xu Gao, Shibo Li, Xin Liu, Shangqian Gao
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33008
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33008
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33008]] TraceQuery: learning what to recompute from full-denoising trajectory in diffusion language models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33008)
  • Keywords: diffusion, large language model
  • Abstract: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) enable flexible generation through iterative masked denoising, but incur substantial inference cost because token representations are repeatedly recomputed across denoising steps. Existing cache-based methods reduce this cost by reusing intermediate results across denoising steps and recomputing only a subset of positions at each step, chosen either on a fixed schedule or by heuristic signals of change. Our trajectory experiments instead point to decoding trajectory as the more relevant quantity. Cached generation remains accurate when it stays close to the full-denoising trajectory, with errors concentrated around unmask-order deviations and reduced as longer reference prefixes are recovered. This suggests a more direct principle: limiting representation error helps, but keeping generation on the full-denoising trajectory is a more direct route to accurate decoding. We therefore formulate efficient dLLM inference as trajectory-guided querying. Rather than broadly repairing representation error, we use full-denoising trajectories to supervise which unresolved positions should receive limited fresh computation. Based on this formulation, we introduce TRACEQUERY, a lightweight sparse decoding framework that predicts a small set of near-future trajectory-relevant positions, queries only those candidates, and lets the backbone select which one to commit from their fresh predictions. TRACEQUERY delivers the strongest quality-computation trade-off among cache-based approaches, sustaining high accuracy with substantially fewer fresh token computations, and generalizes across tasks, model variants, and families. Title: Recursive Semantic Divergence for LLM Agent Consistency

  • Authors: Harshavardhan Abichandani, Penny Chong, Atin Ghosh, Daniel Dahlmeier
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33012
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33012
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33012]] Recursive Semantic Divergence for LLM Agent Consistency(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33012)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Model (LLM) agents with tool use exhibit inconsistent behavior across independent runs from identical states, calling different tools, producing contradictory outputs, or pursuing divergent strategies. This variability compounds over time and undermines deployment reliability. Existing evaluation metrics primarily measure task correctness via completion-based scores and do not capture consistency in agent behavior across executions. Separately, agent behavior is analyzed either at the token level using probability distributions or at the single-response level using semantic clustering, but neither captures how inconsistency propagates across future turns of a trajectory. We introduce Recursive Semantic Divergence (RSD), an unsupervised trajectory-level consistency metric adapted from bisimulation metrics in reinforcement learning. RSD measures the expected cumulative semantic disagreement between two independent rollouts of the same agent from the same state, using natural language inference to detect logical inconsistency. It is defined recursively over future states and approximated by a neural network trained on independent rollouts. The learned metric estimates future divergence from the current state, providing a consistency score at each turn without requiring the full trajectory to complete. When used as a reward signal for fine-tuning, RSD reduces the variation in task progress from 132% to 91% on tau2-bench and from 90% to 44% on toolsandbox, while improving performance across benchmarks and model scales. Training with RSD as an additional reward signal matches supervised fine-tuning performance with substantially lower variance, without requiring ground-truth labels.

Title: SLICE-Mem: Salience-Gated Lightweight Information Curation for Efficient LLM Agent Memory

  • Authors: Xiaobin Chen, Dian Jin, Lin Shu, Defeng Sun, Xiaoming Tao
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33015
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33015
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33015]] SLICE-Mem: Salience-Gated Lightweight Information Curation for Efficient LLM Agent Memory(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33015)
  • Keywords: extraction, generative, large language model
  • Abstract: Long-term memory enables large language model (LLM) agents to maintain personalized and temporally coherent interactions, but repeated LLM inference for memory admission, extraction, and organization introduces substantial cost and latency. We present SLICE-Mem, a lightweight memory framework that combines salience-guided admission and predicate-centered information extraction. A compact classifier identifies user turns worth retaining, and a semantic-dependency-based extractor converts admitted turns into concise memory entries. This online pipeline requires no generative LLM calls, while cross-entry reconciliation is deferred to an offline consolidation stage. We evaluate SLICE-Mem on the weekly subset of Memora and LongMemEval-S using 8B and 30B Qwen backbones for memory construction and consolidation on both benchmarks. Compared with the generative-memory baseline MemOS under the Qwen3-30B configuration, SLICE-Mem's online pipeline achieves 46.60 versus 46.68 FAMA (Forgetting-Aware Memory Accuracy) on Memora Weekly and 56.8% versus 59.3% accuracy on LongMemEval-S, while reducing total LLM token consumption by 92.4% and 99.5%, respectively. These results demonstrate that SLICE-Mem can preserve useful evidence for long-term memory with substantially less reliance on LLM-based memory construction.

Title: AdaptTok-Indic: Script-Aware Adaptive Tokenization for Indic Languages

  • Authors: Binayak Mukherjeea, Moitreya Nandi, Arindam Panja
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33017
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33017
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33017]] AdaptTok-Indic: Script-Aware Adaptive Tokenization for Indic Languages(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33017)
  • Keywords: transformer, segmentation
  • Abstract: Multilingual tokenizers trained on English-dominated corpora often over-segment Indic languages, resulting in longer token sequences and increased tokenization disparity across scripts. We present AdaptTok-Indic, an Indic-specialized extension of adaptive tokenization that combines Unicode-normalized preprocessing with explicit Indic conjunct handling, script-aware routing, and learned byte-level boundary prediction jointly trained with a small Transformer language model. Evaluated on Bengali and Hindi, with additional evaluation on Gujarati, Tamil, and Telugu, AdaptTok-Indic achieves lower token fertility than SentencePiece BPE, an Indic-oriented tokenizer baseline, and an independently implemented MAGNET-style baseline. Across five recorded seeds, mean fertility is 1.432 for Bengali and 1.388 for Hindi, compared with 2.156 and 2.085 for SentencePiece BPE. Recorded downstream scores average 57.69 on evaluated Indic settings versus 50.82 for the SentencePiece BPE configuration. Independent verification runs using the released prototype confirm the same directional trends. The current implementation is a small-scale prototype with documented simplifications, and no statistical significance testing, released-MAGNET comparison, or code-mixed evaluation is performed. These results suggest that Indic-specialized adaptive segmentation can reduce tokenization disparity while maintaining competitive downstream performance.

Title: SUDO: From Scientific Publications to Global Scholarly Discourse

  • Authors: Nipun D. Pathirage, Henrique Santos, Deborah L. McGuinness
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.DL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33018
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33018
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33018]] SUDO: From Scientific Publications to Global Scholarly Discourse(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33018)
  • Keywords: extraction, large language model
  • Abstract: Scholarly Knowledge Graphs (SKGs) support literature search, scientific discovery, and agent-assisted research. Yet, most approaches to SKG construction pre-specify the knowledge unit to be represented, typically named scholarly entities or contribution-level statements, and retain only the publication content expressible through that unit. This choice produces two dominant yet distinct paradigms: concept-centric SKGs that preserve entities and local relations, and statement-centric SKGs that capture high-level discourse in sentences. In both cases, the graph reflects a chosen extraction target rather than how a paper constructs and communicates knowledge. We introduce SUDO, the Scholarly Upper Discourse Ontology, which defines how knowledge elements are organized and related to form a paper's research narrative, namely its scholarly discourse. Using linguistic principles that generalize across fields and tasks, SUDO enables each publication's scholarly discourse to be constructed from the bottom up as a coherent, source-grounded representation. Applied consistently across papers, the same structure connects their research narratives into a global scholarly discourse without discarding local context or provenance. We demonstrate SUDO's capabilities across a comprehensive suite of scholarly knowledge-modeling use cases and performance improvements on a downstream Large Language Model (LLM)-based scientific QA task.

Title: Can Language Models Learn While They Think? Testing Gradient Adaptation Across Architectures

  • Authors: Surya Saka
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33025
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33025
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33025]] Can Language Models Learn While They Think? Testing Gradient Adaptation Across Architectures(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33025)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Letting a language model take gradient steps on the text it is reading, known as dynamic evaluation, lowers its loss on the rest of the document. Its behaviour on hybrid linear-attention architectures, on diffusion language models, and on standard long-context benchmarks has not been measured. We measure it. With one SGD step per 128-token chunk on the MLP output projections of the last quarter of blocks, no training, and a 2k-token window, softmax-attention models (Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-4B, Gemma-4-E4B) lose 5-8% of their next-token loss over a document and outperform the same frozen model given the full 32k-token context. Hybrid Gated-DeltaNet models (Qwen3.5-4B, Qwen3.8-27B) gain under 1% at every learning rate: the linear-attention blocks already perform the adaptation. The mechanism transfers to masked-diffusion models with a larger effect, 8% on Nemotron-Diffusion-8B and 17-20% on DiffusionGemma-26B, once the rate is lowered and updates are clipped. Across RULER, BABILong, LongBench-v2, SCOTUS, HotpotQA, MuSiQue, ContractNLI and citation completion, at 100 or more items on two model sizes, weights updated on the full document recover none of the accuracy lost to context truncation: the adaptation is style and vocabulary, not retrieval. Writing facts with the next-token loss stores the prompt format rather than the fact (30% recall under chat prompting against 80% in context) and costs 36-38 GSM8K points at every dose. Writing a conclusion as behaviour instead, by distilling the model's own conclusion-conditioned judgments into a low-rank adapter, reaches the in-context ceiling (0.83 and 0.88 against 0.84 and 0.88 on 100 games) with GSM8K unchanged, and the conclusion can come from an inquiry loop that found it by asking information-gain questions.

Title: EviGround-CT: Sentence-to-Volume Grounding for Verifying LLM-Generated Chest CT Reports

  • Authors: Ziyuan Li, Juncheng Ni, Feng Li
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33038
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33038
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33038]] EviGround-CT: Sentence-to-Volume Grounding for Verifying LLM-Generated Chest CT Reports(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33038)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Chest computed tomography (CT) reports generated by large language models (LLMs) can describe positive findings that lack localized evidence in the scan. We propose EviGround-CT to verify these findings after report generation. A deterministic extractor identifies positive finding sentences, and a 3D grounding model predicts an evidence mask for each sentence. Using the full sentence retains anatomical location, laterality, and descriptive modifiers in the grounding query. We evaluate report generation on CT-RATE, and grounding and verification on a held-out 100-case ReXGroundingCT subset. Using the available annotations, sentence-grounded verification raises evidence-supported precision (ESP) from 0.263 to 0.365 across 137 extracted in-scope positives, compared with category conditioning. The corresponding unsupported positive finding rate falls from 0.737 to 0.635. The paired case-level bootstrap ESP difference is +0.102 (95% confidence interval: [0.028, 0.181]). The predicted masks associate individual report statements with spatial evidence that can be inspected alongside the CT.

Title: Detection of false positives by human reviewers depends on the face recognition algorithm and its security configuration

  • Authors: Esha Edakoth, John J. Howard, Isabelle M. Shuggi, Yevgeniy B. Sirotin, Robert Wood
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.HC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33045
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33045
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33045]] Detection of false positives by human reviewers depends on the face recognition algorithm and its security configuration(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33045)
  • Keywords: security, biometric
  • Abstract: Integration of artificial intelligence (AI) technology into identity workflows, such as automated face recognition (FR), has enhanced operational capabilities, with FR algorithms achieving super-human scale and accuracy. However, these gains can make human oversight of FR more difficult, raising questions about the effectiveness of these human-AI teams. We measured human reviewers' ability to detect false positive errors made by commercial FR algorithms across varying security configurations. Using a novel dataset, the Difficult Facial Image Comparison Test (DFICT), derived from false positives made by four comparable commercial face recognition algorithms, we measured the ability of 801human reviewers to detect algorithmic false positives across three security configurations, representing operationally relevant biometric thresholds. Our findings show that human reviewers can reduce algorithmic false positives, but performance declines at stricter security thresholds. Further, reviewer performance varied by algorithm indicating that some algorithms are more compatible with human oversight. We suggest that biometric systems should be designed to account for human reviewers' variable effectiveness, particularly in high-security applications. Our findings should be considered during algorithm selection and inform human oversight strategies to minimize AI-related risks in operational FR deployments.

Title: TRACER: An RDC-Triggered Trajectory Recovery Framework for Cyberattack-Resilient Adaptive Cruise Control

  • Authors: Yicheng Yang, Tianyu Liu, Omar Tahri, Haoli Zhao, Yongfeng Dong, Tianyi Li
  • Subjects: cs.CR, eess.SY
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33052
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33052
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33052]] TRACER: An RDC-Triggered Trajectory Recovery Framework for Cyberattack-Resilient Adaptive Cruise Control(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33052)
  • Keywords: attack, robust
  • Abstract: The increasing connectivity of adaptive cruise control (ACC) systems exposes vehicle-state information to cyberattacks, potentially causing irrational car-following behavior and unsafe longitudinal dynamics. Existing trajectory recovery methods generally lack an interpretable criterion for recovery activation and remain vulnerable to attack-induced distribution shifts. To address these challenges, this paper proposes TRACER, a rational driving constraint (RDC)-triggered trajectory recovery framework for cyberattack-resilient ACC systems. TRACER employs a rationality-aware triggering (RAT) mechanism that activates recovery when the observed driving state violates the calibrated RDC bounds. It also introduces cross-branch robust imitation learning (CRIL), which combines a clean branch for learning nominal driving behavior with a noise-enhanced branch for improving robustness to corrupted observations. Cross-branch fine-tuning further aligns the robust recovery policy with the nominal driving-state distribution. Experiments across six cyberattack scenarios show that TRACER achieves reliable recovery triggering and outperforms state-of-the-art recovery baselines in terms of accuracy, stability, and safety. State-space analysis further confirms that TRACER mitigates attack-induced distribution shifts and restores coupled spacing-velocity dynamics, highlighting the effectiveness of robust policy learning for cyberattack-resilient ACC.

Title: Saga: an in silico ontology for a multimodal patient world model

  • Authors: Youcheng Li (Peking University, Isoplex Lab), Fa Tian (Isoplex Lab), Binbin Su (Peking Union Medical College, Isoplex Lab)
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33054
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33054
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33054]] Saga: an in silico ontology for a multimodal patient world model(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33054)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Simulating an individual's disease course requires connecting patient history to future events, clinical timing and disease morphology. Longitudinal prediction and multimodal representations exploit this information, but organizing future readouts around a common patient state and advancing trajectories without real intermediate observations remain central challenges. Here we introduce Saga, a multimodal patient world model that learns a shared state from patient histories to predict clinical events and generate multimodal images. Generated events and times are appended to the history and re-encoded to advance prediction. We studied 461,370 patients with 12 cancer types across 112 centres. Modality-specific paired evaluations linked future images to observed follow-up. With historical images held fixed, removing patient conditioning reduced event F1 from 0.716 to 0.591 and increased lesion-burden error from 11.8% to 21.3%. In free rollouts, eight-step event F1 was 0.672, compared with 0.553 for a longitudinal Transformer evaluated from the same origins, and timing error was lower by 8.7 days. In the internal test set, one-year progression AUROC was 0.882, with values of 0.871 and 0.856 in temporal and geographical external evaluations. These results show that shared patient states connect future phenotypes across observational scales with event-driven trajectory progression, sustaining comparative predictive advantages during rollout and discrimination in external clinical evaluation. Saga makes patient-level disease simulation a multimodal prediction task that can be tested against observed follow-up.

Title: GMoE-FTI: A Universal Facial Template Inversion Method Adopting Genuine Mixture-of-Experts Mechanism

  • Authors: Zixuan Shen, Zhihua Xia, Kaikai Gan, Peipeng Yu
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33055
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33055
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33055]] GMoE-FTI: A Universal Facial Template Inversion Method Adopting Genuine Mixture-of-Experts Mechanism(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33055)
  • Keywords: privacy, protect, biometric
  • Abstract: Face recognition systems have been ubiquitously deployed across daily scenarios. To protect user privacy, mainstream systems store facial templates rather than raw face images for identity verification. However, facial template inversion (FTI) technologies can reconstruct faces from templates, which endangers biometric privacy. Existing FTI methods only fit single type of templates, failing to reconstruct identity-consistent faces from unseen types. To tackle this limitation, we survey mainstream template extractors which can be classified into five categories according the template distribution: unit-sphere, angle-linear, strict unit-sphere, cone-shaped, anuniform hypersphere. Inspired by this observation, this paper proposes GMoE-FTI, a universal FTI framework powered by a genuine MoE mechanism that differs from load-balanced multi-branch alternatives. It follows a three-stage training strategy: (1) independently train five mapping experts (MEs) and a matching module (MM); (2) conduct collaborative inference by weighting each ME's output with MM's output, so as to generate unified latent codes compatible with pre-trained StyleGAN3; (3) perform end-to-end joint fine-tuning over all modules to identity consistency and boost cross-expert coordination. Extensive evaluations on the LFW benchmark validate the superiority of GMoE-FTI against SOTA methods. Evaluated on seven prevailing unseen template types including AdaFace, CosFace, CurricularFace, ElasticFace, SphereFace2, SphereFace-R, and TransFace, GMoE-FTI achieves an average 40% TAR improvement, with average FAPD and FAPC each reduced by 2%, verifying its universal generalization across heterogeneous templates.

Title: Aerial-R1: Reinforcing Aerial Reasoning and Segmentation in MLLMs

  • Authors: Jisheng Dang, Huaiyuan Peng, Yixuan Wang, Kun Wang, Zhang Li, Tat-Seng Chua
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33056
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33056
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33056]] Aerial-R1: Reinforcing Aerial Reasoning and Segmentation in MLLMs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33056)
  • Keywords: robust, extraction, large language model, segmentation
  • Abstract: While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have revolutionized general visual perception, existing methods suffer from severe hallucination in the aerial domain, where extreme scale variations and visual ambiguity abound. Existing Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) paradigms force models to mimic ground truth without modeling uncertainty, often resulting in blind guessing on tiny or unrecognizable objects in aerial scenes. In this paper, we present Aerial-R1, the first aerial MLLM framework to reinforce visual reasoning capabilities for aerial reasoning and segmentation. Drawing inspiration from the reasoning-incentivizing mechanisms of DeepSeek-R1, we find that trustworthy perception stems from consistency across views, establishing a visual parallel to the Chain-of-Thought in logic. To effectively adapt policy optimization for non-autoregressive mask generation, we introduce Hybrid-View Group Advantage-Weighted Regression (H-GAWR). This strategy constructs dynamic groups of high-resolution and low-resolution views, seamlessly bridging group-relative advantage estimation with advantage-weighted policy extraction. By enforcing consensus on clear inputs and strictly penalizing hallucinations on ambiguous ones, Aerial-R1 develops an explicitly incentivized refusal mechanism. Extensive experiments on VSAI-Ref, the first aerial MLLM benchmark dedicated to jointly evaluating dense reasoning and hallucination robustness, demonstrate that Aerial-R1 significantly outperforms SFT baselines in segmentation precision while substantially mitigating fatal hallucinations in low-information scenarios, paving the way for more reliable and trustworthy aerial agents. Title: DRA-SAM: Disagreement-Driven Referential Active SAM for Low-Altitude Drone Referring Segmentation

  • Authors: Jisheng Dang, Huaiyuan Peng, Wencan Zhang, Bimei Wang, Jingwen Zhao, Wenjun Ma, Hong Peng, Bin Hu, Qi Tian, Tat-Seng Chua
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33057
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33057
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33057]] DRA-SAM: Disagreement-Driven Referential Active SAM for Low-Altitude Drone Referring Segmentation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33057)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Referring segmentation in low-altitude drone imagery requires distinguishing small, visually similar instances under complex linguistic and spatial constraints. Global inference may yield multiple plausible masks or leave the intended referent insufficiently represented, motivating selective local re-inference. We propose DRA-SAM, a disagreement-driven framework that allocates an additional inference pass according to competing referential hypotheses and an unresolved state. A lightweight EvidenceScorer estimates beliefs over existing candidates and the unresolved state. A training-free Referential Disagreement LOOK policy balances belief-weighted candidate competition and unresolved-region coverage to select a crop for local re-inference. The same benchmark-adapted SAM3-I backbone processes the selected region, and the resulting evidence updates existing hypotheses and introduces new candidates. Among the compared methods, DRA-SAM achieves state-of-the-art performance on six of seven test metrics on both RIS-LAD and UAVid-RIS, including mIoU and P@0.5-P@0.9, and on all seven RIS-LAD validation metrics. On RIS-LAD, DRA-SAM improves the matched global SAM3-I baseline from 49.55 to 52.34 mIoU and from 46.80 to 58.05 P@0.5. On UAVid-RIS, the independently adapted DRA-SAM achieves 73.26 mIoU and 86.86 P@0.5. Controlled crop-policy comparisons demonstrate the effectiveness of disagreement-guided crop selection, while target-size analysis reveals particularly pronounced gains for aerial targets occupying less than 1% of an image. These results establish referential disagreement as an effective criterion for allocating local inference. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: KDHNet: A Knowledge-Driven Hybrid Network for Limited-Calibration SSVEP Decoding

  • Authors: Zhentao He, Ziwei Wang, Dongrui Wu
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33058
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33058
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33058]] KDHNet: A Knowledge-Driven Hybrid Network for Limited-Calibration SSVEP Decoding(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33058)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Steady-state visual evoked potential (SSVEP)-based brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) achieve high information transfer rates but often require sufficient subject-specific calibration data for reliable decoding. Analytical spatial filters are effective with limited data, but their linear formulations constrain representation capacity. Deep models learn more flexible representations but are prone to overfitting when calibration data are scarce. We propose KDHNet, which combines knowledge-driven analytical processing with data-driven representation learning. After filter-bank preprocessing, cascaded task-discriminant component analysis (TDCA) and task-related component analysis (TRCA) extract discriminative and trial-reproducible components. For inter-subject decoding, a target-guided least-squares transformation aligns source trials using one labeled target trial per class. Extensive experiments were performed on three public datasets, covering intra- and inter-subject protocols, five analysis windows, and multiple calibration ratios. In intra-subject evaluation, KDHNet achieved the highest mean accuracy and information transfer rate in 14 of 15 window settings and all ten calibration-ratio settings. In inter-subject evaluation, KDHNet obtained the best or second-best performance, and the gains were generally larger with less calibration data. Ablation studies indicated that the estimated TDCA projection performed better when fixed than when optimized from analytical or random initialization. Combining fixed knowledge priors with trainable neural modules is effective for low-calibration SSVEP decoding. This framework supports more practical, robust, and plug-and-play BCIs. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: PhaseBreak: Proactive Temporal Protection against Unauthorized Image-to-Video Animation

  • Keywords: protect, diffusion
  • Abstract: Image-to-Video (I2V) diffusion models can animate a single portrait or photograph, enabling shared images to be repurposed for motion synthesis without the subject's authorization. We study proactive visual protection against such use: before an image is released, its owner applies a bounded perturbation that preserves the sharing workflow but disrupts downstream motion generation. Existing spectral disruption objectives globally attenuate temporal representations and therefore provide limited control over subtle or spatially localized motion. Our key idea is that generated dynamics depend not only on temporal feature strength, but also on the order in which feature events occur. We propose PhaseBreak, a white-box spectral retiming protection method that matches intermediate denoiser features to a constrained temporal rearrangement of their clean counterparts. PhaseBreak operates along the true temporal axis and uses a subject-aware mask to confine the protective perturbation to the depicted subject. Evaluations on Stable Video Diffusion and DynamiCrafter show that protected images induce greater short-term motion variation and lower temporal regularity, impeding stable motion customization from the released image.

Title: Hidden Backdoor Attack on Deep Contrastive Clustering

  • Authors: Zifeng Zhou, Shengda Zhuo, Jiahui Huang, Zeyan Li, Jinchun He, Peipeng Yu, Wangjie Qiu, Zhiming Zheng, Shuqiang Huang
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33116

  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33116]] Hidden Backdoor Attack on Deep Contrastive Clustering(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33116)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: Deep contrastive clustering has become a representative paradigm for unsupervised image clustering, yet its security under training-time poisoning remains largely unexplored. This paper investigates a hidden backdoor threat against deep contrastive clustering and proposes HBDCC, a Hidden Backdoor attack for Deep Contrastive Clustering. Here, hidden does not indicate that the trigger is visually imperceptible, but rather that the malicious trigger-cluster association remains behaviorally dormant on clean inputs and is activated only when the trigger appears at inference time. HBDCC poisons only a small fraction of unlabeled training samples and exploits an inherent structural weakness of contrastive clustering: augmentation-persistent triggers can be repeatedly aligned across different views at the instance level and further reinforced by cluster-level consistency objectives. As a result, the poisoned model preserves normal clustering behavior on clean data, while producing severely distorted cluster assignments for triggered inputs. We provide a dual-level analysis of the backdoor implantation mechanism and evaluate HBDCC on ImageNet-10, ImageNet-Dogs, and STL-10 with representative contrastive clustering frameworks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that HBDCC can substantially degrade triggered clustering performance in a dataset-dependent manner while maintaining sufficient clean-data utility to evade standard evaluation protocols. We further conduct comprehensive studies on poisoning ratio, attack scope, trigger type, trigger size, and a lightweight knowledge-distillation-based mitigation strategy.

Title: Physics Informed Neural Networks for Solving the Drift-Diffusion Equations

  • Authors: Luke Ragan, Matteo G. C. Alasio, Enrico Bellotti, Luca Dal Negro
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33133
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33133
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33133]] Physics Informed Neural Networks for Solving the Drift-Diffusion Equations(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33133)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: The drift-diffusion equations are the fundamental building block for the modeling, design, and optimization of modern semiconductor devices. Solutions of the drift-diffusion equations rarely give way to analytical forms, and exhibit strong nonlinearity, with the solutions ranging over several orders of magnitude. These attributes have led to research into numerical solvers, and while many commercial Technology Computer Aided Design (TCAD) packages now exist, they may require long simulation times, exhibit sensitivity to initial guesses, and rely on mesh generation. Further, these solvers are not well suited for inverse problems where the design parameters of a device are retrieved from its desired functional characteristics. To address all these challenges, this work demonstrates the usage of Physics Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) for the solution of the drift-diffusion equations with errors comparable to traditional simulation software using advanced training techniques to solve problems beyond the well-behaved equilibrium regime. For this study, we specifically choose an abrupt PN-junction in both one and two dimensions that features extreme gradients of the carrier concentrations at the Ohmic contacts and an abrupt discontinuity in the doping profile. Using the proposed PINN architecture, we are able to retrieve accurate results for all observables under all bias conditions. Additionally, we apply the PINN solver towards the inverse problem of retrieving doping profiles directly from desired electric field distributions or electrostatic potentials under non-zero applied biases. Our work establishes a powerful PINNs-based approach for semiconductor device modeling with the ability to solve inverse problems under non-equilibrium conditions for microelectronics research and discovery.

Prior attacks mainly rely on keyword-based techniques, which are easily detected, or rule-based methods, which have limited effectiveness. In this work, we introduce TSA, a stealthy tool-spoofing attack that crafts descriptions for malicious tools that are difficult to distinguish from those of existing benign tools. Our user study further shows that even human participants cannot distinguish descriptions generated by TSA from benign ones. Moreover, TSA remains effective against five tool-malware detectors.

Thus, a fundamental gap remains between establishing an age property and revealing information beyond what is necessary to establish that property. Title: Neyman Orthogonal Activation Steering

  • Authors: David Arbour, Erfan Jahanparast, Serina Chang, Avi Feller
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33217
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33217
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33217]] Neyman Orthogonal Activation Steering(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33217)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Activation steering is a popular approach for shifting LLM output toward a desired behavior, and increasingly for generating realistic synthetic data. The standard recipe estimates a steering direction by contrasting LLM activations on prompts with and without a target concept, such as truthfulness. In this paper, we separate the estimand (the steering direction these methods target) from the estimator used to recover it, two notions the literature often conflates. We frame nearly all existing methods as plug-in estimators of the average gradient of an outcome regression, with the binary contrastive label fixed as the outcome. Using this framing, we can immediately incorporate flexible modeling approaches and extend existing methods to allow for continuous outcomes such as log-probabilities or oracle ratings. Flexible ML models that fit the outcome well, however, might not estimate the gradient well, which motivates estimators that target the gradient directly. Drawing on the debiased machine learning literature, we develop a complementary estimator built on the Stein score, the Riesz representer of the gradient functional. Combining the two routes gives an orthogonalized estimator with classical double-robustness properties. We illustrate the framework on activation steering applied to a number of benchmark datasets.

Title: Holo-NN: Holographic Nearest Neighbor Search via Compressive Sensing

  • Authors: Bryant Gipson
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.DB, cs.IR, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33224
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33224
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33224]] Holo-NN: Holographic Nearest Neighbor Search via Compressive Sensing(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33224)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: In the age of AI, privacy-preserving approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) search over high-dimensional embeddings has emerged as a critical yet frustratingly challenging domain, dominated by tradeoffs between speed, privacy, and accuracy. Traditional tree and graph-based indexing structures (e.g., HNSW, ScaNN) rely fundamentally on non-linear operations that are computationally intractable under Fully Homomorphic Encryption (FHE) or blind server-side constraints. In this paper, we introduce Holo-NN (Holographic Nearest Neighbor Search), an algebraic framework that replaces data-dependent branching with a single linear projection. Our key observation is that while distances across a database are dense, neighborhood membership is inherently sparse: only a handful of points ever lie close to a query. By pairing locality-preserving feature transforms with Compressive Sensing, Holo-NN folds the entire database offline into a compact holographic sensing matrix. At query time, encrypted search reduces to a single, non-interactive matrix-vector multiplication---requiring no FHE bootstrapping, no multi-round interaction, and no client-side index state. This turns private retrieval into an embarrassingly parallel, accuracy-tunable computation that runs out of the box on both CPUs and GPUs, scaling efficiently to production databases. We offer a comparison against other state-of-the-art Private ANN algorithms to help situate the method in the broader context of production-grade private retrieval.

Title: Alignment of Autonomous Cyber Defense Agents via Adaptive Policy Shaping

  • Authors: Dena Mujtaba, Bharadwaj Ravichandran, Brian Hu, Arslan Basharat

  • Keywords: security, defense, attack
  • Abstract: Cybersecurity operators increasingly encounter sophisticated and fast-paced attacks, making autonomous cyber defense (ACD) a critical approach for supporting timely and effective decision-making. However, these agents often face critical trade-offs, such as improving network security while inadvertently degrading service availability or violating other operational priorities such as confidentiality and integrity, raising the core challenge of aligning agent behavior with human-specified goals and intent. We formalize alignment for ACD as a policy shaping problem, where a pretrained RL decision-making policy can be adjusted at test time using predicted attributes over agent actions. We also propose an adaptive approach that dynamically controls the alignment--performance trade-off as operational priorities shift during an ongoing cyber attack. We evaluate our method in a simulated cyber defense environment, CybORG, analyzing performance across multiple alignment objectives using the Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability (CIA) information security triad. Our results show that adaptive test-time policy shaping can significantly improve alignment while preserving core cyber defense performance, offering a practical and scalable alternative to training-time alignment methods in RL. Our code is publicly available at this https URL.

Title: FlexA-Mem: Budget-Adaptive Agentic Memory for LLM Agents

  • Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents work in scenarios with very different memory needs. Existing memories are each designed and tested for one kind of scenario. Retrieval memories such as A-Mem miss steps that the current query does not express, summaries drop exact strings, and pairing the two fixes the split of the budget in advance. On LongMemEval and LoCoMo, it matches a dedicated keyword retriever that writes no memory, while memories built on text written by an LLM fall far behind at tight budgets. Ablations show that the timeline carries AppWorld completion, whereas recovery mainly saves steps there and decides accuracy on exact facts. Code is available at this https URL

Title: Individual Fairness via Local Predictive Consistency

  • Authors: Zhouting Zhao, Tin Lok James Ng
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33285
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33285
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33285]] Individual Fairness via Local Predictive Consistency(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33285)
  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: Individual fairness in machine learning requires that similar individuals receive similar predictions. Most existing definitions are pairwise in nature, imposing a Lipschitz constraint that must hold either globally across all pairs of data instances or locally within specified neighborhoods. In practice, however, such pairwise constraints may be overly restrictive and may not directly identify which individuals receive locally inconsistent predictions. We study local predictive consistency as a node-level relaxation of pairwise individual fairness, where each prediction is compared with a similarity-weighted average of predictions in its local neighborhood. This yields an interpretable node-level fairness score and a corresponding regularized learning approach based on local residuals. Since graph-based individual fairness depends critically on the underlying similarity structure, we make the construction of the neighborhood graph explicit and discuss threshold selection through graph-coverage diagnostics. Our approach applies to binary classification, regression, and time-to-event prediction. We further compare this formulation with pairwise constraint-based approaches and analyze the resulting fairness--performance trade-offs.

Title: All-in-One: A Composable Attack on LLM Transformations

  • Authors: Xiaohua Zhan, Kazuki Egashira, Robin Staab, Mark Vero, Martin Vechev
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33298
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33298
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33298]] All-in-One: A Composable Attack on LLM Transformations(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33298)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: Quantization, pruning, and model merging are popular transformations applied to deployment-ready LLMs to improve efficiency and downstream performance. Recent work has shown that these transformations can introduce a security threat: an adversary can train a model that passes safety checks in its pre-transformed state, only turning malicious once it is transformed for deployment. However, prior attacks target one specific transformation (e.g., quantization) and generally rely on transformation-specific techniques, making them difficult to combine in practice. In this work, we introduce a simple, composable attack framework that targets multiple transformations simultaneously and triggers when any one of them is applied by a user. Our key insight is that the effect of each transformation can be approximated as a perturbation to the model weights, allowing us to jointly train the model with a benign loss on the original model and a malicious loss on each simulated transformed model. These perturbations are simple enough to simulate at every training iteration across diverse transformations, making them practically combinable within a single training objective, enabling a unified attack. Through extensive experiments across attack scenarios, model families, and a wide range of deployment-time model transformations, we show that our attack achieves high success rates. This demonstrates that model transformations may be utilized to trigger significantly different behavior from the original model and reinforces the associated security concerns by demonstrating that a single attack can jointly target diverse transformations.

Title: A Deterministic Security Runtime for LLM Agents on MPI and Batch-Scheduled HPC Systems: Consensus-Governed, Hardware-Rooted Supervision of Every Rank

  • Authors: Alfredo Metere
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33318
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33318
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33318]] A Deterministic Security Runtime for LLM Agents on MPI and Batch-Scheduled HPC Systems: Consensus-Governed, Hardware-Rooted Supervision of Every Rank(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33318)
  • Keywords: secure, security, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model agents launch simulations on high-performance computing (HPC) systems, where one sbatch or mpirun can commit thousands of node-hours and a forged result can cost lives. Of 23 surveyed systems, none supervises the running job, and none holds when the control host is compromised. We fix both. Enclawed, our deterministic security runtime for AI agents, has until now served a single user; this work makes it a distributed runtime for parallel HPC and agent swarms. The Enclaweder, our hardware root of trust, keeps a compromised host from becoming a catastrophe. A launch gate decides every launch by policy alone, refusing what it cannot parse. Once the job runs, a swarm chain grants short leases against signed heartbeats and evicts on evidence, and a supervisor around every rank pauses it when its lease lapses and kills it on tampering. Each node signs with its Enclaweder's key, so the host can forge none of it. Enclawed needs no administrator privileges and plugs into existing clusters unchanged, over Secure Shell alone. Supervision leaves the compute loop of a 16-rank LAMMPS job unchanged, on graphics processors too, and adds about 3 s of start-up. Twelve live attacks on Enclaweder-keyed ranks, colluding coordinators included, ended fail-closed. Sixteen agents on a real model attempted 24 launches that planted instructions ordered; the gate denied all 24 and allowed all 108 legitimate ones. Enclawed's core and the HPC Agent Security Benchmark are open source.

  • Abstract: Adversarial training remains one of the most effective defenses against adversarial attacks, but its standard PGD-based training is computationally expensive because adversarial examples are generated for every training sample. However, existing selective techniques typically rely on point-wise difficulty signals such as loss, entropy, margin, or gradients. We show that these point-wise criteria do not capture local instability in a sample's neighborhood. Motivated by this, we propose Augmentation-Guided Adversarial Training (AGAT), a selective adversarial training framework that uses augmentation-induced local variation to allocate samples with unstable neighborhoods for adversarial training. Experiments across different architectures on MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny-ImageNet-200 demonstrate that AGAT achieves comparable or higher robust accuracy while applying adversarial supervision to only 20-30% of the training samples in each minibatch. AGAT also requires less training time than all other adversarial training baselines in the computational cost evaluation.

Title: Gaussian Approximation for Composition and Subsampling of Differentially Private Algorithms

  • Authors: Vitaly Feldman, Moshe Shenfeld
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33333
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33333
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33333]] Gaussian Approximation for Composition and Subsampling of Differentially Private Algorithms(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33333)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: The privacy analysis of differentially private stochastic gradient descent (DP-SGD) relies on bounds for the composition of subsampled mechanisms. The privacy of such a composition is bounded by that of a pair of product distributions, whose privacy loss is a sum of independent terms and has been analyzed via a variety of notions and concentration results. Yet, existing closed-form bounds either lose a logarithmic factor or require restrictive conditions on the noise scale and delicate analysis of subsampling. Further, while it is well known that sums of independent random variables are approximately Gaussian, existing CLT-based techniques give an approximation with a large error in $\delta$. We show that the privacy loss of this pair is approximately Gaussian as long as each step has a small privacy loss except with small probability. Specifically, its privacy loss distribution is close to a Gaussian with the same mean and variance, after truncating the rare large privacy losses. The closeness in both directions is up to a shift of $\varepsilon$ that grows only as $\ln(1/\beta)$ for an additive error $\beta$ in $\delta$ and is a lower-order term in typical applications. In particular, the composition satisfies Gaussian differential privacy up to a lower-order shift of $\varepsilon$ and the additive error $\beta$. We also give a simple way to analyze the resulting variance-based bounds for subsampled mechanisms which relies on the $\chi^2$ divergence of a single step. For the Gaussian mechanism at sampling rate $1/t$ and $t>\ln^{3}(1/\delta)$, this gives the predicted rate for $\sigma\gtrsim\max\{1,\sqrt{\ln(1/\delta)}/\ln(t)\}$, whereas the standard Rényi-divergence analyses need $\sigma\gtrsim\sqrt{t\ln(1/\delta)}/\ln(t)$. We extend these results to random allocation (balls-and-bins sampling), and show that it explains the high accuracy of the connect-the-dots method.

Title: Information-Theoretic Scaling Laws for Generative Diffusion Training

  • Authors: Druv Pai, Dejan Stancevic, Yi Ma, Luca Ambrogioni
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33337
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33337

  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Diffusion models trained on increasingly large datasets sampled from the same data distribution will converge to the same learned distribution. However, in practice, a model trained on finite data depends on the particular training dataset; this dependence is reflected in properties of the model's outputs, such as memorization and sample quality. To understand this dependence through an information-theoretic lens, we study \textit{model information}, a computationally tractable measure of how much samples from the trained model depend on the particular training dataset. We explore its scaling behavior as model size and training dataset size vary. Specifically, we uncover two qualitatively distinct scaling regimes: (1) a nonlinear model information regime marking the transition from pure memorization to imperfect generalization, and (2) a generalization regime characterized by linear scaling of the model information and progressively improved generalization and sample quality. We then posit an information-theoretically principled scaling law to quantify these two regimes, and show that this law yields induced scaling laws for memorization and sample quality. We finally demonstrate that these induced scaling laws extrapolate well outside the fit's domain. Our results demonstrate information-theoretic principles for memorization and sample quality scaling laws in diffusion models. Our code is publicly available on \href{this https URL}{GitHub}.

To resolve this, we introduce the No-False-Positives (NFP) test, a statistical test with a formal guarantee of statistical specificity for identifying features encoding temporal concepts. Title: The Clustering Occupation Kernel (CLOCK) Method for Time Series Arising from Dynamical Systems

  • Authors: Michael L. Wells, Kamel Lahouel, Victor Rielly, Bruno Jedynak
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33354
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33354
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33354]] The Clustering Occupation Kernel (CLOCK) Method for Time Series Arising from Dynamical Systems(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33354)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: We introduce the Clustering Occupation Kernel (CLOCK) method, a novel representation-based framework for clustering time series arising from dynamical systems. CLOCK embeds observed sequences into a reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS) via a time-averaged occupation kernel, enabling trajectory comparison through their RKHS norm. For systems governed by differential equations, this formulation rigorously approximates the kernel mean embedding of the invariant Sinai-Ruelle-Bowen (SRB) measure, meaning our dissimilarity metric approximates the maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) between attractors. To accommodate general time series, we introduce time lags via Takens' Embedding Theorem to reconstruct the phase space. Crucially, CLOCK natively handles trajectories of unequal lengths and irregularly-spaced observations. We validate the proposed metric using spectral clustering, demonstrating superior performance against benchmark methods on both synthetic datasets and a real-world intracranial large-scale electroencephalogram (EEG) dataset. Finally, ablation studies on the Lorenz-96 system are consistent with the theoretical convergence rates of the occupation kernels and highlight the method's strong robustness to zero-mean Gaussian noise.

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Event cameras provide microsecond temporal resolution, high dynamic range, and sparse asynchronous measurements, making them compelling for odometry under fast motion and challenging illumination. Title: LSTM-Style Looped Transformers with Shared KV Caches

  • Authors: Andreas Kirsch
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33360
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33360
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33360]] LSTM-Style Looped Transformers with Shared KV Caches(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33360)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Recurrent transformers reuse weights to perform deep computation with fewer parameters. At width 1024, it uses 40.4% fewer parameters than an untied control at nearly matched forward FLOPs, and reduces persistent cache sets from fifteen to four. Parallel training supplies cache entries from a standard-attention pass, whereas continuation reads entries produced recursively using shared attention. Title: Multiple Importance Sampling for Guided Speculative Inference

  • Keywords: robust, large language model
  • Abstract: We study an extension of the Guided Speculative Inference (GSI) algorithm for test-time-scaling of large language models (LLMs) to a novel setting where an ensemble of smaller LLMs proposes draft reasoning steps which are later verified by a larger base model and a process reward model. Using the established framework of multiple importance sampling (MIS), we introduce weighting schemes to aggregate samples from a diverse collection of proposal distributions. Title: How Does Post-Training Shape LLM Value Preferences?

  • Authors: Yang Liu, Shaoyang Xu, Masahiro Kaneko, Chengzhi Zhong, Wenxuan Zhang, Chenhui Chu
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33371
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33371
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33371]] How Does Post-Training Shape LLM Value Preferences?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33371)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: During post-training, large language models (LLMs) learn value preferences from corpora that may contain imbalanced proportions of opinions associated with different demographic groups. Amplifying these imbalances may further underrepresent some groups' opinions. However, how opinion mixtures shape value preferences across post-training paths remains unclear. To answer this question, we conduct controlled experiments using post-training corpora with different opinion mixtures and five post-training paths based on SFT, DPO, and GRPO. We find that post-training amplifies opinion imbalances differently across post-training paths. Once value preferences are amplified, whether further post-training can remove them depends on both the post-training method and the opinion mixture in the corpus. More concerningly, a model's stability under interventions depends on whether further post-training changes its value preference. Moreover, these value preferences are transferred to student models even when distillation uses corpora with no explicit opinion questions. These findings suggest that both the opinion mixture in post-training corpora and the choice of post-training method should be considered when developing, evaluating, and reusing LLMs. Our code and data are available at this https URL.

Title: One Call Ahead: Packed Self-Speculative Decoding with Diffusion Drafting

  • Authors: Yuxiao Chen, Changxiao Cai
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33379
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33379
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33379]] One Call Ahead: Packed Self-Speculative Decoding with Diffusion Drafting(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33379)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) language models generate tokens sequentially, so generating long outputs is costly. Speculative decoding mitigates this by using a lightweight drafter to propose multiple tokens for the target AR model to verify in parallel, while a diffusion-based drafter can produce an entire draft block in one forward pass. In models with AR and diffusion modes, one network both drafts and verifies, but drafting still waits for verification, so the standard schedule needs two sequential model calls per iteration. Existing one-call schedules avoid the wait but incur input size quadratic in the speculative width. Motivated by this efficiency bottleneck, we introduce Packed-SS, a self-speculative decoding schedule that verifies and drafts in a single model call with input size linear in the speculative width, while preserving the target model's distribution. The key idea is to let the diffusion drafter run one call ahead: while verifying the current draft, the model simultaneously constructs the next draft from an older verified prefix and keeps its unresolved suffix for the next iteration. Under a mild monotonicity assumption on the drafter, we further show that Packed-SS requires no more model calls than the standard two-call decoding in the greedy setting. Experiments on Nemotron 3B/8B and SetDLM 1.7B show higher throughput and lower mean wall time than both baselines under greedy decoding.

Title: Combinatorial Lock for Trojan-Resilient AI Engines via Hash-Assisted Spectral Filtering

  • Authors: Soumita Chatterjee, Sudip Ghosh, Tamal Ghosh, Bhargab B. Bhattacharya, Hafizur Rahaman
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AR, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33437
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33437
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33437]] Combinatorial Lock for Trojan-Resilient AI Engines via Hash-Assisted Spectral Filtering(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33437)
  • Keywords: defense, attack, watermark
  • Abstract: Multi-trigger backdoor attacks, in which heterogeneous trigger mechanisms (e.g., patch, blend, warping, and the latent-domain Spy-Watermark) coexist within a single poisoned model, cannot be addressed by existing shortcut-based defenses that assume a single, static attack mechanism. Current multi-trigger remedies (BU+FT, BU+ANP, and BU+ABL) rely on backdoor unlearning, which incurs high training costs and loses effectiveness at low poisoning ratios. This paper proposes Combinatorial Lock, a training-free, reference-calibrated defense that reduces multi-trigger identification to a combinatorial problem over binary activation signatures. This formulation is the key novelty: it yields a multi-trigger defense that is training-free, trigger-oblivious, and directly implementable in hardware, since its decision rules can be evaluated with integer and logic operations. The framework combines anomaly-gated spectral scrubbing, which removes the malicious subspace only from inputs identified as anomalous, with a hash-compressed combinatorial signature verified by an ensemble of detection experts, including a compound-attack-aware expert for multi-trigger inputs. A joint threshold-shrinkage search identifies an operating point ($\tau$ at the 97.1st percentile, $\gamma = 0.78$) that reduces the attack success rate (ASR) to as low as 0.1% while preserving 97.8% clean accuracy. Evaluation on six models (ResNet18, VGG16, MobileNet, AlexNet, ViT-Small, ViT-Base) shows strong generalization: near-100% ASR drops to single digits across poisoning rates of 1-10% and 1-9 simultaneous triggers, with only gradual degradation in clean accuracy. ASIC and FPGA implementations confirm practicality, adding a consistent 19-20% latency overhead across all evaluated architectures and establishing Combinatorial Lock as a hardware-native approach to multi-trigger backdoor mitigation.

Overall, the results show SCAPE-T isolates conceptual structure from lexical/syntactic confounds better than existing methods, and generalises across two held-out domains.

  • Abstract: Decisions about heat, frost and flooding are made locally, yet reanalyses and climate models resolve tens of kilometres. Here we introduce CD-FFM, a functional flow-matching model that treats the high-resolution field as a continuous random field that one model can query at any location and resolution. Title: Five Architectures, One Shared Space Cross-Model Representational Alignment Without Weight Merging

  • Authors: Oladimeji Anthonio, Dimeji Olawuyi, Oloruntoba Ajayi, Temilola Aderemi
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33495
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33495
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33495]] Five Architectures, One Shared Space Cross-Model Representational Alignment Without Weight Merging(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33495)
  • Keywords: robust, transformer
  • Abstract: Combining language models is usually done by averaging weights, which works only when the models share a common starting point. We ask whether five frozen, independently trained models with different internal designs (four transformers from four organizations and one state-space model) can be related through their activations alone, with no weight merging. Using permutation tests, an independent replication probe set, depth sweeps, pooling ablations and confound checks, we find statistically robust correspondence between how the five models organize the same text. It is strongest at each model's final layer and weaker for the state-space model, whose weakest pairing is still 35-43 times chance. We use this to build a shared space with Generalized Procrustes Analysis, in which no model is a fixed reference. At the final layer, mean held-out retrieval accuracy over the twenty ordered model pairs is 0.91 to 0.93 on two probe sets (chance 0.03), beating pairwise linear maps (0.82 to 0.85), but the space is worse than those maps in the middle layers. It transfers to ordinary English of unfamiliar grammatical form and fails on mathematical and logical notation. We then test whether the space supports a router that picks among the five models on gradeable tasks. Across four task types we find no case where routing beats always using the strongest model, and we report this as a negative result. A correctness signal that looked strong turned out to be category membership in disguise. All experiments ran on one laptop without a GPU.

Title: FedSEAM: Federated Continual Learning of LLMs via Subspace Expert Agreement Merging

  • Authors: Fatema Siddika, Abhijit Chunduru, Zilinghan Li, Ali Jannesari, Ravi Madduri
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33496
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33496
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33496]] FedSEAM: Federated Continual Learning of LLMs via Subspace Expert Agreement Merging(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33496)
  • Keywords: protect, federate
  • Abstract: Federated continual learning trains a model on a sequence of tasks across several clients without sharing their data while retaining knowledge of earlier tasks. Sparse mixture-of-experts models support this setting by adapting selected parameters on a frozen backbone. However, clients learn different expert content as tasks evolve, making it difficult to share knowledge across clients without disrupting task-specific and client-specific specialization. Matching tile coordinates alone does not establish whether their learned content is compatible. We present FedSEAM, a federated continual learning framework that organizes task knowledge into sparse experts and selectively shares their content across tasks and clients. On each client, task-aware tile selection allocates capacity to a new task, while a subspace-based compatibility test determines which existing expert tiles to reuse and where to introduce fresh capacity. On the server, Subspace Expert Agreement Merging (SEAM) compares client updates as subspaces and moves each client towards consensus only along agreed-upon directions, leaving the rest of its update unchanged. We identify two remaining sources of forgetting, routing drift and merge erasure, and address them with three protections, one of which exactly preserves tile components in a selected prior-overlap subspace at every merge. We evaluate FedSEAM on TRACE using Qwen3-4B and Llama-3.2-3B, with component-wise ablations and studies of data size to assess its balance between learning new tasks and retaining prior knowledge.

  • Authors: Yangyong Deng, Pengyu Zhang, Shaoqiu Song, Xiaotao Huang, Jian Wang
  • Subjects: cs.CV

  • Abstract: Automotive millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar has attracted increasing attention for semantic segmentation in autonomous driving, owing to its robustness under adverse weather and lighting conditions. However, existing methods typically treat the guard bandwidth as learnable parameters, without explicitly characterizing the frequency response of the PKC operator. Title: Towards a Learnable Retriever for Textual Graph Question Answering

  • Authors: Kaifeng Jin, Yuchen Yan, Zhichen Zeng, Xiao Lin, Lihui Liu, Yinglong Xia, Hanghang Tong
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33511
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33511
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33511]] Towards a Learnable Retriever for Textual Graph Question Answering(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33511)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Textual graph question answering (GraphQA) and graph-based retrieval-augmented generation (GraphRAG) have gained increasing attention as a way to ground large language models (LLMs) on structured knowledge. Despite extensive efforts, existing methods still face two key gaps. The first is an architectural gap: existing methods typically fine-tune an LLM-based generator, but do not also make the retriever itself a learnable LLM for graph evidence selection within one end-to-end retrieve-then-generate pipeline. The second is a retrieval-supervision gap: existing retrievers rarely use gold answer signals to supervise evidence selection. As a result, they may fail to recall evidence nodes required for reasoning, resulting in insufficient coverage of the gold answer set; meanwhile, naive attempts to boost recall often introduce excessive irrelevant information that compromises compactness and overwhelms the LLM. To address these gaps, we propose TaLenT, an LLM-based end-to-end retriever-generator framework for textual GraphQA. To bridge the architectural gap, TaLenT pairs a learnable LLM-based retriever for evidence selection with a fine-tuned LLM-based generator within one unified retrieve-then-generate pipeline. To bridge the retrieval-supervision gap, we leverage gold answer signals as retriever supervision. Specifically, we stratify graph nodes into gold answers, answer-related nodes, and irrelevant noise. We then adopt a weighted pseudo-label loss to prioritize gold answers and preserve answer-related nodes for coverage, while discarding irrelevant noise for compactness. Experimental results across three GraphQA datasets demonstrate that TaLenT consistently improves downstream performance.

Title: One Model, Two Paths: Balancing Single-Pass and Looped Performance in Language Models

  • Authors: Jiarun Shen, Lei Zhao, Xiao Li
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33517
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33517

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Repeating Transformer layers can improve language-model performance without adding parameters. A pretrained model can be adapted to support both single-pass and looped inference by reusing middle-layer weights for additional computation. Because both paths share weights, training to improve looped predictions can degrade single-pass quality. We study joint training strategies using weighted losses on both outputs, with guidance from the frozen original model. Joint supervision substantially reduces single-pass degradation compared to looped-only training, while preserving looped gains over ordinary continued training. Most of this benefit comes from adjusting the single-pass loss weight, with guidance from the frozen original model further improving the looped output. With validation-selected weights, our method preserves single-pass quality within a predefined tolerance of ordinary continued training while improving looped predictions.

Title: Implicit Beliefs as Scalable Process Rewards for Strategic Persuasion

  • Abstract: Training large language models (LLMs) for multi-turn strategic persuasion faces two coupled obstacles: (i) realistic simulation of psychologically grounded persuadees, and (ii) dense, faithful supervision for credit assignment in strategic dialogues. Our work offers a promising paradigm that can provide effective process supervision for strategic dialogue.

Title: Patch-Derived or Register Tokens? Comparing Variable-Length Image Tokenizers

  • Authors: Philipp Schenkel, Tobias Schürmann, Omer Sahin Tas
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33540
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33540
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33540]] Patch-Derived or Register Tokens? Comparing Variable-Length Image Tokenizers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33540)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Image tokenizers convert pixels into compact latent sequences for efficient generative modeling. Most approaches arrange latents on a downsampled two-dimensional grid, tying sequence length to image resolution. We compare two variable-length strategies: computationally efficient patch-derived tokens, which inherit explicit spatial organization, and more demanding register tokens, which have no predefined spatial layout. Our reconstruction results reveal that patch-derived tokens perform substantially better under extreme compression, whereas register tokens achieve slightly better reconstruction quality at intermediate budgets. At high token budgets, both approaches achieve similar reconstruction quality. For downstream generative tasks, register tokens outperform patch-derived tokens. Adding representation alignment with external pretrained visual encoders substantially improves the generative performance of patch-derived tokens, allowing them to match or outperform register tokens depending on the token budget. We further find that both approaches learn spatially localized representations. Patch-derived tokens largely retain their explicitly imposed spatial organization, while register tokens develop an implicit two-dimensional spatial structure despite having no predefined layout. Representation alignment reduces this spatial organizational constraint, enabling individual tokens to exert a stronger non-local influence.

We performed ablations with end-to-end training and data consistency (DC) enabled or disabled. VarNet-Seg achieved the highest Dice at every undersampling level. At eight-fold acceleration, Dice was 0.22 for VarNet-Seg, 0.14 for SENSE-Seg and 0.16 for VarNet-Seg (no DC). The paired difference from SENSE-Seg was 0.08 (95% CI, 0.04-0.11). With full sampling, VarNet-Seg and RSS-Seg performed similarly (0.40 vs 0.39). Patient-level AUROC decreased from 0.75 with full sampling to 0.63 at eight-fold acceleration; corresponding RSS-Seg AUROCs were 0.76 and 0.64. The proposed multicoil network improved prostate lesion segmentation under simulated undersampling compared to conventional models. Matched ablations supported contributions from both end-to-end training and data consistency. Prospective evaluation is needed to establish whether this approach can be used clinically in accelerated prostate DWI.

Title: Nowcasting aerosol particle size distributions from PM2.5 with physics-regularized flow matching

  • Authors: Zhi-Song Liu, Jenni Köykkä, Wenqing Peng, Ammar Kheder, Petri Clusius, Michael Boy
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33557
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.33557
  • Copy Paste: [[2610.33557]] Nowcasting aerosol particle size distributions from PM2.5 with physics-regularized flow matching(https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33557)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: The mass concentration of particles smaller than 2.5um (PM2.5) is cheap to measure and widely monitored, whereas the particle number size distribution (PSD) requires costly instruments and is measured at only a few research stations. We explore whether recent generative deep learning models can nowcast the PSD: from the PM2.5 record and eight meteorological parameters over a 24h window, a model generates an ensemble of plausible hourly size distributions for the same window, reflecting that one mass can arise from many size distributions. Flow matching performs best overall; its guided variant has the lowest continuous ranked probability score (CRPS) at both sites. A constraint based on a reduced aerosol general dynamic equation (GDE) lowers the CRPS by 5.5-11.5% at the training site and reduces errors after transfer between sites. Transfer remains harder than local estimation, identifying training on large multi-station data sets as the next step towards PSD estimates at unmonitored locations. This benchmark establishes physics-informed generative models as a promising route from routine air-quality monitoring to size-resolved aerosol information.

Title: GeoLift: Grounding Video Spatial Reasoning in Explicit 3D Maps

The reconstructed 3D map enables explicit computation of spatial relations and multistep routes, and provides geometric evidence for estimating distances and sizes. With all model weights frozen, GeoLift improves Qwen3-VL-4B's average score on VSI-Bench from 55.4% to 64.1%, including an increase in route-planning accuracy from 30.9% to 53.6%, and achieves 59.8% accuracy on MindCube. These results show that connecting frame-level object grounding through a shared 3D map can improve complex spatial reasoning without task-specific training.
